# Registry changelog

Reverse-chronological log of changes to [`dependencies.yml`](dependencies.yml), plus FS.GG.Kit
republishes that change the fleet materialized by [`repos.yml`](repos.yml) without changing a
dependency row — the FS-GG cross-repo contract & dependency registry. Its human projection is
[`../docs/registry/compatibility.md`](../docs/registry/compatibility.md).

**Protocol.** Every change to `dependencies.yml`, and every FS.GG.Kit republish, **prepends one dated entry** at the top of
the Entries list below (newest first) and sets the file's `updated:` date to match. Entries
follow a loose `HEADER (owner; refs): body` grammar — name the contract/coherence id(s)
touched, the owner, and the issue/ADR refs. One entry per registry change keeps PR diffs
reviewable and avoids the merge conflicts the former single-line comment caused across the
org's parallel sessions.

This file replaces the ~42 KB single-line trailing comment that used to live on
`dependencies.yml`'s `updated:` field (freeze migrated verbatim below; see .github#129 /
review M4). The typed validator (`fsgg-sdd registry validate`) never saw that comment, so
no gate behaviour changes — this is purely how humans record the log.

## Entries

- **2026-08-12** — **release: coherent-set cut, `coord-engine` 0.23.0 → 0.50.0, `FS.GG.Kit` 0.49.0 → 0.50.0, `FS.GG.Drivers` 0.18.0 → 0.50.0** (owner `github`; [.github#2402](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2402), [.github#2409](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2409), [.github#2442](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2442), [.github#2445](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2445)/[PR #2455](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/2455)). The FIRST real coherent-set release: `.github#2402` gave FS.GG.Kit, FS.GG.Drivers and coord-engine one shared `$(FsggCoherentSetVersion)` MSBuild property (source-only, merged 2026-08-11); `.github#2409` (PR #2441, merged 2026-08-12) added a sibling-tag precondition to each of `release-kit.yml`/`release-drivers.yml`/`release-coord-engine.yml` so none of the three can publish without the other two at the same commit; `.github#2445` (PR #2455) added `scripts/check-release-coherence.py`, a post-publish gate proving trio completion and dual-feed agreement. This entry is that mechanism's first real exercise: `kit/v0.50.0`, `drivers/v0.50.0` and `coord-engine/v0.50.0` were pushed together in one `git push` at commit `248435375cc6be0554e5c93552fd5cf44c128fd8`, each workflow's sibling-tag precondition passed, and all three ran (runs [31607040051](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/31607040051) kit, [31607040112](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/31607040112) drivers, [31607040033](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/31607040033) coord-engine), each reporting `Your package was pushed.` on both the org GitHub Packages feed and nuget.org. Every non-signature, non-psmdcp payload entry is byte-identical between the two feeds for all three packages (verified by downloading both artifacts and comparing per-entry SHA-256 after unzip). `python3 scripts/check-release-coherence.py` reports `ok: no coherent-set completion gap found` against this exact release — the first non-vacuous run of that gate. A REAL isolated restore proves consumability, not a source build: `dotnet tool install FS.GG.Coord.Cli --version 0.50.0` succeeds and `dotnet fsgg-coord-engine --version` reports `0.50.0.0`; `dotnet add package FS.GG.Kit --version 0.50.0` and `... FS.GG.Drivers --version 0.50.0` both restore from nuget.org into a fresh console project's `project.assets.json`. `scripts/dashboard-tick.py --package FS.GG.Coord.Cli --version 0.50.0` (run in CI with a real credential) reached all 7 receivers, 0 undetermined, 0 findings — every receiver's dashboard `manual job` box was already ticked, so every no-op is genuine. `coord-engine`'s `<Version>` moved without any `Protocol.fs`/`Options.fs` change (`git diff coord-engine/v0.23.0..248435375cc6be0554e5c93552fd5cf44c128fd8 -- src/FS.GG.Coord.Core/Protocol.fs src/FS.GG.Coord.Cli/Options.fs` is empty) — the 0.23.0→0.50.0 jump is entirely the coherent-set convention, not a caller-visible behavior cut; the packed nuspec's `<releaseNotes>` states this explicitly and carries the full prior-version prose history. **Rollout note (this item's own AC, `.github#2409` DEC-005 / SB-005):** every one of the 7 receivers reads `fs.gg.coord.cli` at `0.22.1` (`FS.GG.Net` at `0.21.1`) as of this release (checked live 2026-08-12 against each receiver's `.config/dotnet-tools.json`) — a 28–29 minor jump to `0.50.0`, far beyond any bound `.github#2396`'s permitted-lag decision contemplates (not yet implemented; that item is `Blocked on: human/decision`). No plausible lag bound covers a jump this size, so a coordinated fan-out bump PR to all 7 receivers, in the shape `.github#2249`'s own AC1/AC2 describe, is owed and tracked as a post-merge obligation of this item rather than performed inside this registry-only PR (cross-repo work is outside `.github#2409`'s declared `Paths:`). `registry/dependencies.yml`'s `coord-engine` row is flipped to `0.50.0` on both `version` and `package-version` per this evidence — full detail in that row's own comment.

- **2026-08-10** — **release: `coord-engine` 0.22.1 → 0.23.0** (owner `github`; [.github#2345](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2345), [.github#2175](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2175)/[PR #2349](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/2349), [.github#2135](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2135)/[PR #2327](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/2327), [.github#2306](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2306), [.github#2245](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2245) acceptance 6, [.github#2264](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2264)/[PR #2271](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/2271)). Also ships two DEFECT-class fixes that were blocking `engine-freshness`: #2306 (a `widen`/`set-paths` call refused with `OVERLAP` no longer mutates the held declaration) and #2245 acceptance 6 (`RepoScope.resolve` maps the rostered short id `sir` to `EHotwagner/S.I.R.`, closing a scheduling-namespace split between the board's Repo Scope option and the engine's `--repo`/reservation keys), plus #2264's lifecycle Status projection (PR #2271, board reconciliation converges Project Status from live PR/review/CI/delivery facts through one writer). All 32 shared payload entries between the two feeds are byte-identical (only `.signature.p7s` — public-only — and the per-push `core-properties` psmdcp metadata differ, as expected). Org GitHub Packages feed SHA-256 `4d400e7437cc0489fc55d6806b7adc66ad6907e1e01916642e3fd2a02bdb6769` (live `2026-08-10T23:04:32Z`); public nuget.org signed SHA-256 `4c70b4fe6c57397e0f580865be5a09eff500ace0f3632ede5eaf937bf3fec431` (indexing lagged the org push by roughly 45 minutes — confirmed indexed, downloaded and hashed directly rather than assumed). A NuGet.org-only tool install reports `0.23.0.0`; delivery reached 7 receivers with zero undetermined or findings; `EHotwagner/S.I.R.` correctly received no tick (`receives: []`, non-participant per PR #2326 — resolved at runtime by `scripts/dashboard-tick.py:104`'s roster query, no workflow edit needed). Post-publication `engine-freshness` is green (0 unreleased commits). **⚠ The packed `PackageReleaseNotes` for this version is incomplete, not false**: it names the `driver --events`/`--cursor` vocabulary and both defect fixes but never names the `review` verb, even though `review` ships in this exact artifact. This registry's `dependencies.yml` `coord-engine` row and this entry are the complete, authoritative record of what 0.23.0 ships; burning a version number on byte-identical code to correct understated prose was evaluated and declined — see .github#2345 for the full reasoning and the (separately filed) `.github#2360` process-enforcement gap this release also surfaced.

- **2026-08-10** — **registry: activate `fs-gg-workspace-template` (console/web/fable-game/fable-bindings) and `game-skills`** (owner `github`; refs [.github#2070](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2070), epic [.github#2067](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2067), [ADR-0071](../docs/adr/0071-two-web-workspace-providers-one-template-package.md)/[ADR-0072](../docs/adr/0072-console-and-fable-bindings-are-separate-workspace-providers.md)/[ADR-0073](../docs/adr/0073-plain-http-with-explicit-dtos-replaces-fable-remoting.md)). Two new contract rows, both independently re-verified against nuget.org rather than trusted from a closing comment. `fs-gg-workspace-template` (owner `templates`) pins `FS.GG.Workspace.Template` **0.8.0** — the exact version FS-GG/FS.GG.Templates#349 published (tag `fs-gg-templates/v0.8.0` → `fef78f5b`) and every one of Templates' four `providers/*.providers.yml` descriptors and the real first consumer (EHotwagner/S.I.R.#138, merge `b17ac33b`) name — re-downloaded nupkg SHA-256 `11b57ac61e2c3eadaefae0d20ae427e4ca6f7593585cf79e5f6e89565b93c473` matches #349's own reported hash. nuget.org already serves a newer 0.8.1 (unrelated hardening cut, Templates PRs #397/#399/#401/#403) that is deliberately **not** pinned here — no descriptor or consumer has re-pinned to it yet, and advancing ahead of them would be the registry-says-what-it-wishes-were-true failure this log's own entries repeatedly warn against. `game-skills` (owner `game`) pins `FS.GG.Game.Skills` **0.7.0** — published by FS-GG/FS.GG.Game#552 (PR#554, merge `7fa79f29`) and proven materialized by SDD's production scaffold materializer in FS-GG/FS.GG.SDD#817 (PR#819, merge `aa1d6d4c`); the re-downloaded `fs-gg-game-fable` `SKILL.md` digest `443a82d24a0b4bbd21f4499b06f6e3d12b95a36a858f3880b414b74cae1a5c50` matches the row already at `registry/skills.yml:207` (no skills.yml edit needed — it was already current). `game-sim-core`'s `consumers:` gains `templates` (`fs-gg-fable-game` consumes the published `fs-gg-game-core-fable-lockstep-v1` profile at the unchanged 0.13.0 pin, independently confirmed as S.I.R.'s exact `Directory.Packages.props` pin) alongside two new dependency edges (`templates -> game` via `game-sim-core`, `sdd -> game` via `game-skills`). `minimum-fsgg-sdd` for the four new identities is registered at 0.6.0, mirroring FS.GG.Templates' own descriptors exactly (registry mirrors the descriptor, never leads it) — this is a known-understated floor for `fable-game` specifically (its materializer correctness needs `FS.GG.SDD.Cli` ≥ 1.0.0, the first published build containing SDD PR#819's merge commit `aa1d6d4c`; `v0.32.0...aa1d6d4c` compares `ahead_by=2 behind_by=0` and `aa1d6d4c...v1.0.0` compares `ahead_by=12 behind_by=0`), flagged in the row's own comment and filed as a distinct FS.GG.Templates finding rather than silently corrected here. `new-sdd-workspace` stays at the released 0.9.0 — its `--template` selector merged via .github#2069/PR#2074 (commit `31c4700f`) but is unreleased; packing/releasing it is epic #2067 rollout phase 6, reserved for the maintainer's sign-off and explicitly not performed by this activation. `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/registry/compatibility.md` (via `scripts/generate-projections`) updated to match; both `scripts/generate-projections --check` and `fsgg-sdd registry validate` report clean. Both new package-bearing contracts are mapped in `scripts/registry_packages.py`'s `CONTRACT_PACKAGES` (`fs-gg-workspace-template` → `FS.GG.Workspace.Template` — the id that actually exists on the feed, not the contract id, since the package was renamed before its first publish; `game-skills` → `FS.GG.Game.Skills`), with `tests/feed-coherence/run.sh`'s synthetic registry/feed fixtures extended to carry both so the gate's own ORPHAN check (every `CONTRACT_PACKAGES` mapping needs a contract in the registry under test) stays green. `registry/driver-skill-manifest.json` regenerated (`scripts/generate-driver-manifest --write`) after this entry's `.agents`/`.claude` `publishing-and-deployment` skill edits. **CORRECTION** (same PR, before merge, CI red on head `824c4c2f`): the two paragraphs above describe the FIRST cut's reasoning and are WRONG about which field the "don't advertise past what's consumed" discipline applies to. `check-feed-coherence.py` (`feed-coherence.yml`) asserts `package-version` equals the literal newest live on the feed for every package-bearing contract, unconditionally — that is a different field from the `dependencies:` edges (e.g. `templates -> rendering`), which are what legitimately lag a consumer's actual adoption. Pinning `fs-gg-workspace-template` at 0.8.0 and `game-skills` at 0.7.0 by consumer-adoption reasoning was therefore wrong for THIS field, and the gate correctly reddened BEHIND the feed on both. Corrected to the live newest: `fs-gg-workspace-template` → **`FS.GG.Workspace.Template` 0.8.1** (tag `fs-gg-templates/v0.8.1` → `286c2a43`; PRs #397/#399/#401/#403, FS.GG.Templates#386 — a real fix closing a `**/.nuget/**` leak into generated products; byte-diffed against 0.8.0: only the five `template.json` files changed, no identity added or removed; nupkg SHA-256 `102c72364ce0018b34950774b86ee12d0f6da43e0de36d609f14bfbdae5c5d21`). `game-skills` → **`FS.GG.Game.Skills` 0.8.0** (tag `skills/v0.8.0` → `7b8d2447`; two unrelated docs-only commits since 0.7.0; nupkg SHA-256 `e058642649510e730da168d97610fe96d12805870112127227a64b15e9b1b9f0`; the `fs-gg-game-fable` `SKILL.md` digest is byte-identical to 0.7.0's, so SDD#817/PR#819's materializer proof still holds). `scripts/registry_packages.py`'s `CONTRACT_PACKAGES` mapping and `tests/feed-coherence/run.sh`'s fixtures were unaffected by this correction (both already keyed on package identity, not version). Independently re-verified live against the org feed with a real token after the fix: `python scripts/check-feed-coherence.py registry/dependencies.yml` reports `ok` for all 11 package-bearing contracts including both new rows.

- **2026-08-08** — **republish: `FS.GG.Kit` 0.43.0 → 0.44.0** (owner `github`; refs [.github#2137](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2137)). The mandatory, source-bound delivery-route decision is receiver-visible coordination behaviour, so this is a MINOR release; publication remains a post-merge dual-feed obligation.

- **2026-08-08** — **registry: name the complete `fs-gg-ui-template` provider-descriptor subject, not one historical member** (owner `github`; refs [.github#2247](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2247), [FS.GG.Templates#383](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Templates/issues/383), [FS.GG.Templates#99](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Templates/issues/99)). `minimum-fsgg-sdd.provider-descriptor` formerly named only `providers/rendering.providers.yml`, although all five `FS.GG.Templates/providers/*.providers.yml` descriptors mirror `minimumFsggSdd`. The field now names that enumerable subject, so the next pin advance starts with the whole mirror set rather than discovering four members from a red downstream gate. This is deliberately a prose subject rather than a YAML list: the typed registry validator does not read this advisory field, whereas Templates' required `composition` check runs `scripts/check-provider-floors.py` and enumerates the descriptors directly. Adding a list here would therefore create a second membership authority that no validator checks. The stale active wording around closed Templates#99 is retained only as history; the field now names the live enforcement and its required check. `grep -n 'provider-descriptor\\|mirror' registry/dependencies.yml` found no other `provider-descriptor` field to sweep. Registry shape and its compatibility projection are unchanged; `architecture-map: unaffected` is declared in the PR because no repository, boundary, coherent-set axis, or §5 contract picture moves.

- **2026-08-05** — **release: `coord-engine` 0.21.0 → 0.21.1 (publish-before-flip step 2), and the fleet engine pin advances 0.19.0 → 0.21.1** (owner `github`; refs [.github#2251](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2251)/[PR #2255](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/2255), FR-007). `FS.GG.Coord.Cli` 0.21.1 was cut from annotated tag `coord-engine/v0.21.1` at merged `main` commit `b8f437ccf2f8f867290f18a4a863d0a3cce49f55`. [Release run 31013271481](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/31013271481) locked-restored, tested, packed **once**, installed the produced tool for its production DECIDE smoke, pushed that single archive first to GitHub Packages and then to nuget.org through OIDC, and reached all seven coordination-kit receiver dashboards (`reached 7 · undetermined 0 · findings 0`). Both archives were **downloaded after publication** and compared **entry-wise**: 33 shared payload entries, **zero** content differences, nothing present only on the org feed, and exactly one entry only on the public feed — nuget.org's own `.signature.p7s`. The org archive SHA-256 is `46cb2ef31e7c03a7b4d690a46e7bd3654b330540e058bb9404b310e4d988aaf6` (2458853 B, [GitHub Packages record 1101901447](https://github.com/orgs/FS-GG/packages/nuget/FS.GG.Coord.Cli/1101901447)); the public signed archive SHA-256 is `2be8f1c067a032c99384b7cbe5d1e1d711fda423cc2640d5c273a7350baf8962` (2471943 B, [nuget.org](https://www.nuget.org/packages/FS.GG.Coord.Cli/0.21.1)). The GUID-named OPC part `1eb24aed960a4a12a98f59bccc419f85.psmdcp` is **identical in both**, which is positive evidence that nothing was re-packed between the two pushes — [.github#2240](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2240) records that this name is regenerated per `dotnet pack` invocation, so a re-pack would appear as one org-only plus one public-only entry rather than as a whole-file hash difference. **PATCH, and 0.21.0 is why that needed measuring rather than asserting**: that cut was a MINOR *despite* an untouched `Protocol.fs`, because this contract's `surface:` names the CLI **verb surface** first and independently of the exit-code contract and it added the `diff-audit` verb. This cut adds none — `Protocol.fs|.fsi` **and** `Options.fs|.fsi` are all four untouched across the four commits. Measured on the two PUBLISHED binaries, per the 0.20.1 precedent: 0.21.0 and 0.21.1 emit **byte-identical** `command-contract --json` (8326 B) and `facts --json` (46378 B) — covering all 13 verdict tokens, 7 `take` exit codes, 7 `landable` exit codes, blocker states, board statuses and the snapshot-document contract. The confound is guarded rather than assumed: the binaries are genuinely different code, and on one snapshot carrying a **closed, unstamped** row with no body, published 0.21.0 exits 0 with `"verdict":"green"` while published 0.21.1 exits 1 refusing `items[0].body: required field is missing`. Behaviour diverges; the declared surface does not. The cut carries [.github#2225](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2225)'s DEFECT-class fix — closing is the *middle* of an item, not its end, and three read paths used `state = Closed` as a stand-in for terminal where facts are selected, so `who` reported empty for a live claim, `batch` reserved nothing (a second worker could be dispatched into the holder's tree, [.github#1858](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1858)'s class), and `delivery` blamed the item's own `Paths:` line for the reader's blindness — plus [.github#2221](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2221)'s review-marker repairs: a *quoted* marker no longer invalidates every genuine marker on a PR, a *misplaced* one is refused by name instead of dropped and then reported as an accepted chain, and the misplacement rule now covers the two field-less kinds (escalation, repair-phase) where it had been vacuous. `check-engine-freshness` had turned **RED** on `main` over that defect-class drift and now reports **0 unreleased engine commits**. **`.github`'s own canonical engine manifest moves in the same transaction**: `dist/dotnet/.config/dotnet-tools.json` pinned `fs.gg.coord.cli` at **0.19.0**, two published versions behind, so `check-engine-pin` was red. 0.20.0, 0.20.1 and 0.21.0 all published without advancing it, because `release-coord-engine.yml` never touches it and `engine-pin-coherence.yml` runs its live job on `push: main` rather than `pull_request`, so the red is invisible from any release PR. Pinned **after** publication, never before, per FR-007. **This does NOT deliver the engine to the fleet, and no claim here should be read as saying so.** Since ADR-0068 / [.github#1615](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1615) (`f76da773`, 2026-07-28) this file is *"simply no longer copied into anybody else's tree"* (`registry/repos.yml:585-586`); the coordination-kit roster carries 8 rows and none of them is a `dotnet-tools.json`. Each receiver pins the engine in its **own root** `.config/dotnet-tools.json` — the manifest `scripts/fsgg-coord:251` actually reads — and Renovate's nuget manager is what moves those. Measured on 2026-08-05, after this publication: FS.GG.Templates, FS.GG.Governance and FS.GG.SDD are all still at **0.14.0**. So [.github#2249](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2249) — every receiver pinning 0.14.0 against a registry declaring far newer — is **NOT** closed or advanced by this release; its target simply moves from 0.21.0 to 0.21.1. (`check-engine-pin.py`'s own docstring still describes the retired byte-copy arrangement as its subject, which is what invited the wrong reading; that overclaim is filed as [.github#2257](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2257) and is not repaired here.) Generated projections and the driver manifest were refreshed; architecture-map shape is unaffected.

- **2026-08-05** — **release: `coord-engine` 0.20.1 → 0.21.0 (publish-before-flip step 2)** (owner `github`; refs [.github#2231](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/2231)/[PR #2232](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/2232), FR-007). `FS.GG.Coord.Cli` 0.21.0 was cut from annotated tag `coord-engine/v0.21.0` at merged `main` commit `099c717bef9e2b23d2c5249088192cad2cd7d342`. [Release run 30980703001](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/30980703001) locked-restored, passed Core 613 and CLI 592, packed **once**, installed the produced tool for its production DECIDE smoke, pushed that single archive first to GitHub Packages and then to nuget.org through OIDC, and reached all seven coordination-kit receiver dashboards (`reached 7 · undetermined 0 · findings 0`). Both archives were **downloaded after publication** and compared **entry-wise**: 33 shared payload entries, **zero** content differences, nothing present only on the org feed, and exactly one entry only on the public feed — nuget.org's own `.signature.p7s`. The org archive SHA-256 is `3969e8072f0863d0d6e92d00238bcbc204ab8120b48b606ce2e80df6f316d674` (2453295 B); the public signed archive SHA-256 is `295fe41a31cbca2899e51882f822a637e3fbcb8514d5fec09154bc4766728a3e` (2466384 B, [nuget.org](https://www.nuget.org/packages/FS.GG.Coord.Cli/0.21.0)). **MINOR, and the wire-surface reading would have got this wrong.** `src/FS.GG.Coord.Core/Protocol.fs` is untouched by all 15 commits, which invites a PATCH — but this contract's `surface:` names the CLI **verb surface** first and independently of the exit-code contract, and the cut **adds a verb**. Measured on the published binaries rather than argued from the diff: an org-feed install of the previously published 0.20.1 answers `unknown command: diff-audit`, while a **nuget.org-only** install of 0.21.0 (cleared sources, public feed alone) self-reports `0.21.0.0` and answers with that verb's own usage line — so the verb addition is observable to a receiver on the read path five of the six restore from, not merely present in the source diff. - **2026-07-29** — **publish-before-flip step 2: `coord-engine` 0.14.0 → 0.15.0, the WIRE SURFACE reaches the fleet, and the pin with it** (owner `github`; refs [.github#1906](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1906), [.github#1680](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1680), [.github#1671](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1671), [.github#1615](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1615), [.github#1762](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1762), [PR #1912](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/1912), FR-007). Advances `coord-engine` `version` and `package-version` together. **MINOR, on this row's own rule** — set at 0.12.0 (*"a 0.x minor is the only spelling available for it"*) and restated at 0.13.0 and 0.14.0: a 0.x line has no major to take, so an observable-to-callers behaviour change is spelled MINOR here, and several of these are breaking in the ordinary sense. **Ten commits shipped; eight are observable to callers, in six independent groups, any one of which forces the minor.** **THIS RELEASE CARRIES THE WIRE SURFACE, AND THAT IS THE DIFFERENCE FROM 0.14.0.** **#1680** (`06c1887`) — `landable` answers a PR that is **not open** with exit **10** and one of two verdict words (`merged` / `closed`), a code and a vocabulary it could not produce before. GitHub nulls `mergeable` on merge, so the four-row match read a merged PR as `Computing` and returned `pending`/exit **7** — the one code the contract defines as *worth retrying* — for the most terminal state there is; `landable --wait` therefore spent its entire 600s budget (30 tries × 20s) on a settled fact, every time, and the caller that meets this most is the **recovery path** re-gating a PR whose worker died between merge and stamp. Live: #1675 `pending`/7 after 600s → `merged`/10 in **0.69s**. Exit 10 is deliberately not 3, because `merged` is a *success* whose next act is to **stamp**. **THE #353 COLLISION GATE — three commits, one surface** (`widen`, `set-paths`, `overlap --active`): **#1740** (`474ef9e`) stopped deciding reservation from the board's `Status` **column**; **#1779** (`071780e`) re-keyed the candidate set on the **lock** rather than any projection of it, because `claim` exits green with that column unwritten in **four** ways and the cache/queue fixes reached only two — measured either side of one call on the live board, **24/27/31 GraphQL points and `DISJOINT` (wrong) → 0/0/0 and `OVERLAP` (right)**; **#1792** (`bfcdefb`) made `Scan.snapshot` and the gate agree by asking `reserver` (a lapsed lease still reserves) instead of `winner` (it does not). A `widen` 0.14.0 **permitted** now **refuses** — and that is the fix worth taking the release for: there is no CAS on a file, so a wrong `DISJOINT` was **final**, and two workers really did edit one file this way on 2026-07-28. **#1794** (`06b8ac7`) — `Reads.openIssues` **fails closed**: an element with no numeric `number` was dropped silently and an absent or ill-typed `body` was read as `""`, which parses as *"declares nothing"* and collides with nothing, so **both failed open into the gate above**. **#1668** (`c6489d1`) — `who` gains a fourth row state `UNDETERMINED` with its own `.state` word in `--json`; `Ok []` on an In-progress row used to render as `UNCLAIMED` with a *"working outside the protocol"* accusation, printed **32 seconds before** a `--force` deleted a live marker on the same item (consumer residue is [#1897](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1897), deliberately not fixed here). **#1677** (`fc1a8aa`) — `FSGG_CLAIM_LEASE_MIN` is read **at all**; it was named across this repo and honoured by no line of code, so a worker who exported it now gets a **different lease**, and a malformed value exits 1 where 0.14.0 silently used 120. **#1823** (`b3f0e2e`) — `add` writes `Status: Backlog` where the column was left unset; fourteen invisible rows were filed that way on 2026-07-28 and **every one was found by accident**. Also shipped: **#1666** (`7019939`) tells a **secondary** (abuse-detection) rate limit from the primary budget — it fires on burst concurrency with the primary counter untouched and **never appears in `/rate_limit`**, the reset was read off the *primary* window (hence *"resets in ~6m"* before a retry that succeeded in seconds), and `Retry-After` was read nowhere; **#1654** (`5fbde62`) is comment-only. Release from merge `04c6cbddbe69f8195204808eb60ae2a868ad4e01`, annotated tag `coord-engine/v0.15.0`, run [30426098578](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/30426098578) green end to end. **BOTH FEEDS WERE RESOLVED BY DOWNLOADING THE ARTIFACT, NOT BY READING A LISTING AND NOT BY TRUSTING THE RELEASE JOB'S GREEN** (epic [#266](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/266)): org GitHub Packages record `1077061411` @ 05:48:23Z, and nuget.org's **flat container** polled cache-busted (`?cb=<ns>` + `Cache-Control: no-cache`) — the blob **404'd for twelve polls** and turned 200 on the **thirteenth at 05:53:13Z**, while `index.json` **still ended `0.14.0` on every one of them, including the poll that served the package**. The index and the blob disagreed at the moment of verification and **the blob was believed**; a listing-only check would have reported this release missing while it was demonstrably downloadable. Excluding only nuget.org's appended `.signature.p7s`, all **31** payload entries match byte for byte — zero per-entry hash mismatches, zero entries present on the org feed but absent publicly (org archive SHA-256 `c3cdca5863cfd73a63db77dec274a8e1c3826e441bdc007acd3f1b81a06311d5`, public `a347bf5787ae242f06ac720fd3735d9735749af01efa992cb5b0e85d91f326ba`); one `dotnet pack`, two pushes, no re-pack. Both nuspecs read `<version>0.15.0</version>` and record `commit="04c6cbddbe69f8195204808eb60ae2a868ad4e01"` — binding the **served** package to the merge rather than to the tag, because a tag is not a publish. **THE TRANSACTION IS WIDER THAN THE REGISTRY**, because a registry flip alone cannot close it: `check-engine-pin.py` compares `dist/dotnet/.config/dotnet-tools.json` against the **feed, never this row**, so `engine-pin-coherence` goes red the instant the publish lands. The pin is bumped 0.14.0 → 0.15.0 in this same PR. **AND HERE THIS TRANSACTION IS NARROWER THAN ITS OWN SPECIFICATION — A CORRECTION WORTH RECORDING.** [#1906](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1906) step 6 (and every `coord-engine` row before it) says the pin is *"one of the six hashed `kit:` sources"* and therefore obliges an **FS.GG.Kit republish**. **That stopped being true on 2026-07-28.** [#1615](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1615) / ADR-0068 took `dist/dotnet/.config/dotnet-tools.json` **off the kit fabric** at kit 0.18.0 (14:22Z) — *after* the 0.14.0 flip this transaction was copied from — so the coupling the item reasoned from no longer exists. Measured on the flip branch with the pin at 0.15.0: `check-kit-published-coherence` exits **0** against published kit **0.21.0** and `repos.sh validate` exits **0** with `registry/repos.lock` **unchanged**. No kit republish is owed by this bump, and `src/FS.GG.Kit/FS.GG.Kit.csproj` + `registry/repos.lock` — two of the item's six declared `Paths:` — were correctly **not touched**. (Kit 0.21.0 was cut separately by [#1904](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1904) / PR #1909, run [30425123292](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/30425123292), for a `pnext-item/SKILL.md` change unrelated to this release.) **WHAT THIS RELEASE ACTUALLY CLOSES**: `check-engine-freshness.py` had been **red on `main`** since #1680 merged, because that commit changed `src/FS.GG.Coord.Core/Protocol.fs` and `scripts/generate-projections` had already emitted exit **10** into `pnext-item/references/command-contracts.md` and its `.agents` mirror — shipped to all seven receivers in **FS.GG.Kit 0.20.0**. So the org's own worker-facing documents described a verb contract **no published engine implemented**, which is [#1075](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1075)'s condition and #1680's own defect still live for every receiver. Both gates are now green on `main`: `check-engine-freshness.py` → 0, `check-engine-pin.py` → 0. The standing release-debt story is [#1671](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1671). Generated compatibility and architecture projections regenerated (one version cell each); architecture shape is unaffected.

- **2026-07-28** — **publish-before-flip step 2: `game-sim-core` and `game-scene-adapter` 0.12.0 → 0.13.0; activate `fs-gg-game-core-fable-lockstep-v1`** (owner `game`; refs [Game#526](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Game/issues/526), [Game PR #531](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Game/pull/531), release run [30395301449](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Game/actions/runs/30395301449), FR-007). `FS.GG.Game.Core` now packages a bounded Fable compatibility declaration, generated canonical cases, and a 442-byte oracle (SHA-256 `9c0d7128f7b9558ef7e985618b1165199f83a0bb4af7533b175cc95b340c4a24`). - **2026-07-28** — **publish-before-flip step 2: `coord-engine` 0.13.0 → 0.14.0, and the fleet pin + kit with it** (owner `github`; refs [.github#1761](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1761), [.github#1671](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1671), [.github#1762](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1762), [.github#1615](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1615), [.github#1731](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1731), [PR #1763](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/1763), FR-007). Advances `coord-engine` `version` and `package-version` together. **MINOR, and the rule is this row's own** — set at 0.12.0 (*"a 0.x minor is the only spelling available for it"*) and restated at 0.13.0: a 0.x line has no major to take, so an observable-to-callers behaviour change is spelled MINOR here. **Six commits shipped and THREE force it independently, so patch was not defensible.** **#1646** (`b047b1a`) — *a session is not a credential*: `verifyHeld` granted the `Held` capability whenever the live marker's worker id matched the id `--worker` **named** and `twinSession` reported no twin — and `twinSession` reports a twin only when both sessions are known AND differ, so a caller passing another worker's id matched on **both** legs, because every subagent of one Claude Code session shares one session id. Enforced now at both of `Held`'s doors (`verifyHeld` and `claim`), so `release`, `heartbeat`, `widen`, `done`'s lock-drop and the ADR-0041 chore lock are covered by construction rather than by each command remembering an `if`; a call 0.13.0 permitted now exits non-zero. Review of the same fix found a second hole it would otherwise have left open: `claim --force --worker <a third party>` evicted a live holder **and signed [#1620](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1620)'s theft notice with an innocent worker's id** — falsifying the accounting rather than merely bypassing it. **#1628** (`0beb561`) — the blocking count ranks over the **whole board** rather than the `--repo`-scoped candidate set, so `batch` and `next` can select a **different item** for the same board; scheduling order is what every caller of those verbs consumes. **#1541** (`cb5e291`) — `reconcile --apply --json` now **parses**, so the org's one mutating reconcile verb gains a machine projection the parser previously rejected outright. Also shipped: **#1679** (`656506d`) the chore offer's board read is FRESH — it read the board through the 90-second scheduling cache, so a `CLASS-PROJECTION-LAG` chore **survived the very write that discharged it** and was re-offered to the next worker (fixed with a third `ReadIntent` rather than by borrowing `Reconciling`'s warrant); **#1644** (`e21c8d5`) `BLOCKER-CLEARED` respects the `Blocked on: human/…` sentinel it was promoting over; **#1636** (`debbce6`) `KitDigest` hashes two runtime skill roots after ADR-0067 §5 retired `.codex/skills`, which changes the computed digest for the same input tree. **THIS RELEASE CARRIES NO WIRE SURFACE, AND THAT IS WHY IT WAS OWED** — `git log coord-engine/v0.13.0..8d4e7b2 -- src/FS.GG.Coord.Core/Protocol.fs` is empty, so `check-engine-freshness` was **green over all six** and each sat unshipped beneath it. That is exactly the gap [#1671](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1671) names, and it made this cut an obligation rather than a repair: **three of the six are fixes to the claim protocol**, found on 2026-07-27 when seven workers ran concurrently against it, and every worker vendors `scripts/fsgg-coord`. `.github` builds the engine from source (ADR-0034 decision 2) so its own workers already had them; the seven receivers restore the package and had **none** of them. Release from merge `8d4e7b28d4611116c908b2df29b20f1d0f8b7f0c`, annotated tag `coord-engine/v0.14.0`, run [30349601781](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/30349601781) green end to end. **BOTH FEEDS WERE RESOLVED BY DOWNLOADING THE ARTIFACT, NOT BY READING A LISTING AND NOT BY TRUSTING THE RELEASE JOB'S GREEN** (epic [#266](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/266)): org GitHub Packages record `1073981905` @ 10:10:27Z, and nuget.org's **flat container** polled cache-busted (`?cb=<ns>` + `Cache-Control: no-cache`) until the blob turned 200 on the **fourteenth** try. **The index and the blob disagreed at the moment of verification and the blob was believed**: `index.json` still ended `0.13.0` on every one of those fourteen polls *including the one that served the package*, so a listing-only check would have reported this release missing while it was demonstrably downloadable. A stale 404 is not a failed publish, and a stale hit would not have been a successful one. Excluding only nuget.org's appended `.signature.p7s`, all **31** payload entries match byte for byte — zero per-entry hash mismatches and zero entries present on the org feed but absent publicly (org archive SHA-256 `89260fea16ce4bbba9119313a17461e4eddea3ad6db9fc158afe8d467a4b5aa8`, public `842b311451f7ab4d62a191b09797991ca990f98e4b157537ad9fc9993b9958b3`); one `dotnet pack`, two pushes, no re-pack. Both nuspecs read `<version>0.14.0</version>`, and the nuspec records `commit="8d4e7b28d4611116c908b2df29b20f1d0f8b7f0c"` — binding the **served** package to the merge rather than to the tag, because a tag is not a publish. **THIS TRANSACTION IS DELIBERATELY WIDER THAN THE REGISTRY**, because a registry flip alone cannot close it: `check-engine-pin.py` compares `dist/dotnet/.config/dotnet-tools.json` against the **feed, never this row**, so `engine-pin-coherence` goes red the instant the publish lands and no `dependencies.yml` edit can green it. The pin is therefore bumped 0.13.0 → 0.14.0 in the same PR, `scripts/repos.sh relock` re-derived `registry/repos.lock` (**exactly one** of the nine kit digests moved — the pin), and because the manifest is one of the hashed `kit:` sources **FS.GG.Kit is republished 0.15.1 → 0.16.0** with it. A **minor**, on this republish class's own precedent: 0.11.0, 0.12.0 and 0.13.0 each took a minor for a pin bump because the pin decides *which engine binary every receiver `exec`s*. Whether the engine pin should ride the kit fabric at all is [#1615](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1615), **already decided as option (c) — the coupling is KEPT** — so this fan-out is expected and no decision is re-opened. **THE FAN-OUT IS STATED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED**: all **seven** `coordination-kit` receivers (SDD, Rendering, Governance, Templates, Game, Audio, Net) are now behind on both the engine pin and the kit, and they were **already** behind at kit 0.15.1 — tracked in [#1731](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1731), not re-filed here. Receiver bump PRs were deliberately **not** landed by this item. **ONE DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED IN PASSING, ITS GATE FILED NOT BUILT**: `<PackageReleaseNotes>` still opened *"0.12.0 — BREAKING…"* because the 0.13.0 cut moved `<Version>` and left the notes untouched, so **the 0.13.0 nuget.org listing announced the previous release's break and none of its own seven commits** — including #1620's `claim --force` semantics change. The block is rewritten for 0.14.0 in PR #1763; the missing gate is [#1762](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1762) and is deliberately left open. Generated compatibility and architecture projections regenerated (one version cell each); architecture shape is unaffected.

- **2026-07-28** — **`FS.GG.Kit` 0.15.0 → 0.15.1: the kit's own `scripts/skill-view` was unlintable, and only a receiver could see it** (owner `github`; refs [.github#1718](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1718), [PR #1722](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/1722), [.github#1696](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1696), [.github#1676](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1676), [FS.GG.Game#514](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Game/pull/514)). **THIS ENTRY FLIPS NO `dependencies.yml` ROW, AND THAT IS NOT AN OVERSIGHT** — `FS.GG.Kit` has no row there (`check-feed-coherence.py` compares 9 package-bearing contracts and the kit is not among them), so no `version`/`package-version` scalar moved and `registry-feed-coherence` was never red. It is recorded here anyway because a kit republish is a *fleet* event this log is the only human-readable place for: it restales all seven `coordination-kit` receivers at once, and `registry/repos.lock` — which does live under `registry/` — advanced with it. The strict reading of this file's protocol is "one entry per `dependencies.yml` change"; this entry is deliberately outside it rather than silently absent, and the mismatch is filed, not papered over. **WHAT WAS WRONG.** `scripts/skill-view` (a `kit:` row since 0.15.0, [#1696](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1696)) `source`s `lib/args.sh` and `lib/roots.sh` through **relative** `# shellcheck source=…` pragmas and carried no file-scoped `# shellcheck source-path=SCRIPTDIR`, so shellcheck resolved them against **its own working directory** and linted the file with **both libraries unread** — `SC1091` twice from the repository root, which is how every gate in this org invokes it. **A RECEIVER FOUND IT, AND THE REASON IS STRUCTURAL.** `FS.GG.Game`'s shell gate is the only one in the org that runs at `-S info` and treats an unfollowed `source` as a red in its own right; its 0.15.0 bump was red on that and **nothing else** (28 of 29 checks green, run [30321396512](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Game/actions/runs/30321396512) job `90157884273`). The other six receivers had already landed 0.15.0 **carrying the same broken file**, because none of them can see the defect — a green that is only green because the subject is invisible is not evidence, which is epic [#266](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/266) exactly. This repo's own `scripts/lint-shell.sh` runs at `-S warning` and `SC1091` is an `info`, so the gate that owns the org's shell was blind to a defect it shipped; that is **[#1719](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1719)** and **no severity floor was moved in this release**. **WHY A REPUBLISH AND NOT SEVEN ONE-LINE PRs.** `scripts/skill-view` is kit-materialized, so a directive written into a receiver's tree is reverted by its next `FsggKitMaterialize`; the fix has exactly one correct home. **THE POSITION IS PART OF THE FIX, MEASURED NOT INHERITED** — with the pinned shellcheck 0.11.0, a directive placed next to the `source` lines binds to the *next command only*: it clears the `args.sh` finding and leaves the `roots.sh` one standing. It is file-scoped only above the first command. **PATCH, and every clause is checkable**: the member set is unchanged at 28, every destination, mode and materialize property is unchanged, and `skill-view`'s runtime behaviour is identical — the only bytes that move are a comment block in one `kind: client` member. Release from merge `6550f5814e837ea40fd1f9327fb2b9073e385ca3`, annotated tag `kit/v0.15.1`, run [30338265913](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/30338265913). **BOTH FEEDS WERE RESOLVED BY DOWNLOADING THE ARTIFACT, NOT BY READING A LISTING AND NOT BY TRUSTING THE RELEASE JOB'S GREEN**: org GitHub Packages record `1073429994` @ 07:25:05Z, and nuget.org's **flat container** — whose index still ended `0.15.0` while the blob 404'd — was polled cache-busted (`?cb=<ns>` + `Cache-Control: no-cache`) until it turned 200 at **07:30:03Z**, thirteen tries; a stale 404 is not a failed publish, and a stale hit would not have been a successful one. Excluding only nuget.org's appended `.signature.p7s`, all **38** payload entries match byte for byte (org archive SHA-256 `7fe077c070e5996f51bfb5e2476d4bd6b76e981a3b2bd966c81a6e754d6fab76`, public `9ee38b9fe68b4fbdb928f292a9469cb96f6246286bd1b58fe7c338ab2c171cda`); one `dotnet pack`, two pushes, no re-pack. Both nuspecs read `<version>0.15.1</version>`, and the **published** `kit/client/skill-view` hashes `c0bfe2dc8a4e8001ecc9370ba0d3560520ec385ce685673e0dfbee0f72cc8be7` on both feeds — equal to the `registry/repos.lock` row on `main`, so the artifact the fleet restores is provably the fixed file and not merely a version number that moved. `kit-published-coherence` on `main` went **red at 07:28:11Z** (run [30338529602](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/30338529602), the expected window between merge and nuget.org serving) and **green at 07:30:17Z** (run [30338664994](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/30338664994)) once the blob landed. **THE FAN-OUT THIS SETS IN MOTION IS THE PRICE, AND IT IS STATED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED**: `registry/repos.yml`'s `kit:` block warns in place that editing a kit source obliges a republish and a seven-receiver fan-out. Six receivers — SDD, Rendering, Governance, Templates, Audio, Net — were on 0.15.0 and are now **behind at 0.15.1**; Game was on 0.8.0 and is the one this release exists to unblock. **all six are reachable by Renovate**, read from `default.json` rather than from any repo's in-tree comment — the only rule that disables a pin file, `packageRules[4]`, is a positive allow-list of the four `build-config` receivers (Game, Governance, Rendering, SDD), where those files are materialized from the kit and a bump PR structurally cannot merge; Audio and Net are not in it, and Templates' inline `.proj` pin became readable when [#1564](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1564) added `/\.proj$/` to `nuget.managerFilePatterns`. **CORRECTION, recorded rather than quietly rewritten:** this entry as first merged (`7d5e2b9`) claimed Audio and Net "will not be proposed a bump and need a hand-bump". That was **wrong**, and it was wrong because it repeated a comment in `FS.GG.Net`'s own `Directory.Packages.props` — *"That arm is currently DEAD here (.github#1552) … Until #1552 lands this pin must be bumped BY HAND"* — instead of reading the rule. **#1552 has landed**, so that comment is stale and is filed for repair; `FS.GG.Audio`'s equivalent comment is correct. The fan-out is tracked in [#1731](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1731).

- **2026-07-27** — **publish-before-flip step 2: `coord-engine` 0.12.0 → 0.13.0, and the fleet pin + kit with it** (owner `github`; refs [.github#1673](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1673), [.github#1667](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1667), [.github#1671](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1671), [.github#1658](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1658), [.github#1615](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1615), [PR #1678](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/1678), FR-007). Advances `coord-engine` `version` and `package-version` together. **MINOR, and the rule is this row's own.** The change-class precedent is not in `docs/` — it was set on this very row at 0.12.0: *"a 0.x minor is the only spelling available for it"*, because a 0.x line has no major to take, so an observable-to-callers behaviour change is spelled MINOR here. Two of the seven released commits qualify under exactly that precedent, and either alone forces it: **#1620** (`78408d7`) made `claim --force` really STEAL a live claim — the flag had been read in one place only, `claim`'s #516 one-item-per-worker pre-check, so its implemented meaning was "let me hold two items" while `adopt`'s refusal and the usage block advertised "let me take someone else's" — a command-semantics change; and **#1562** (`4f78e10`) took prose off `next`'s `$(…)` **stdout contract**, where the empty arm used to yield the string "nothing schedulable right now." at exit 0. Patch was not defensible for either. Also shipped: #1588 severity vocabulary (`2aeae77`), #1598 scheduler rank (`c7f7538`), #1575 `landable` deriving its must-have-reported set from the PR's own `mergeable_state` (`3d5caa9`), #1649 (`3fc96ca`), #1651 (`d52362c`). **`.github#1667`'s wire-surface correction rode this release on purpose** — `src/FS.GG.Coord.Core/Protocol.fs` is what `check-engine-freshness.py` measures, so touching it reds the gate until a publish; it was filed rather than fixed in PR #1662 and landed here, the window where that costs nothing. Release from merge `bf5509a9fa99d627185f825803b4aa282dbea9d9`, annotated tag `coord-engine/v0.13.0`, run [30308622600](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/actions/runs/30308622600) green end to end. **Both feeds were resolved by DOWNLOADING the artifact, not by reading a listing**: org GitHub Packages record `1072353556` @ 21:54:09Z, and nuget.org's flat container — whose index lagged ~5 minutes and was polled cache-busted until the blob turned 200 at 21:57:58Z rather than reading the stale 404 as a failed publish. Excluding only nuget.org's appended `.signature.p7s`, all **31** payload entries match byte for byte (org archive SHA-256 `8fd6bdc1a60c88c30d6172741aecebbb38355bdd838ced61baaa841a0f3e0afc`, public `a6763c10e411cffd6cb9ca38dfd284eebd6ad0ed103380e7591bb8f2ad8eff2c`); one `dotnet pack`, two pushes, no re-pack. **This transaction is deliberately wider than the registry**, because a registry flip alone cannot close it: `check-engine-pin.py` compares `dist/dotnet/.config/dotnet-tools.json` against the **FEED, never this row**, so `engine-pin-coherence` goes red the instant the publish lands and no `dependencies.yml` edit can green it (.github#1658 corrected exactly that causal story). The pin is therefore bumped 0.12.0 → 0.13.0 in the same PR, `scripts/repos.sh relock` re-derives `registry/repos.lock`, and — because the manifest is one of the six hashed `kit:` sources — **FS.GG.Kit is republished 0.12.0 → 0.13.0** with it. That republish was owed twice over: this release also edited `.claude/skills/pnext-item`, another kit member, via the regenerated `landable` exit-code projection. Whether the engine pin should ride the kit fabric at all is **[.github#1615](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1615)**, **decided today as option (c) — the coupling is KEPT** — so this fan-out is expected and accepted, and no decision is re-opened here.

- **2026-07-27** — **publish-before-flip step 2: `fsgg-contracts` 7.2.0 → 7.4.0 (both scalars; one flip covers two source bumps)** (owner `sdd`; refs [.github#1659](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1659), [FS.GG.SDD#737](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.SDD/issues/737)/[PR SDD#749](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.SDD/pull/749), [FS.GG.SDD#742](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.SDD/issues/742)/[PR SDD#753](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.SDD/pull/753), FR-007). Advances `version` and `package-version` together. **The publish was already done — this item made none, and none was owed.** `.github#1659` was FILED for 7.2.0 → 7.3.0 and RETARGETED to 7.4.0 by worker `smew-7cf6`; the retarget was re-verified rather than trusted, and both local gates named 7.4.0 unprompted before any edit — `check-source-coherence.py` reported "`version` is '7.2.0' but the SOURCE on FS-GG/FS.GG.SDD is '7.4.0'" and `check-feed-coherence.py` reported "BEHIND the feed — declares '7.2.0' but newest on the org feed is '7.4.0'". Flipping to 7.3.0 would have satisfied neither, so **this flip subsumes 7.3.0**, which this row never pinned — the same shape as 4.0.0 → 5.0.1 subsuming 5.0.0 and 7.0.0 → 7.2.0 subsuming 7.1.0. **7.3.0** (merge `da07830c3cb3cafd9118fc5b6b8e2c68ab388d03`, release run [30296723600](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.SDD/actions/runs/30296723600)) closes an ADR-0014 §Decision 3(c) fail-open: `Fsgg.SkillMirror.sha256` hashed text a caller had already decoded with `File.ReadAllText`, whose UTF-8 decoder substitutes `U+FFFD` before hashing, so `0xFF` and `0xFE` collided under one digest; it adds the new DU `BodyRefusalReason`, `decodeBody` and `sha256Bytes`, and does **not** redefine the digest (`sha256Bytes = decodeBody >> Result.map sha256`), so no recorded manifest digest changes and no consumer migration is owed. **7.4.0** (merge `4383b820e8bf8046e24780f3323edd0659200db4`, release run [30299196197](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.SDD/actions/runs/30299196197), conclusion success) packs `api-surface/*.fsi` in the `.nupkg` — [FS.GG.Rendering#782](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/782)'s producer half, which FS.GG.Contracts had never done. **Both minors were measured here from the publish point, not inherited** (this row's own [#1010](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1010) rule): across `67253fa6a54d34cb54a526a92c84ae9ae7b302b3` → `4383b820e8bf8046e24780f3323edd0659200db4`, restricted to `src/FS.GG.Contracts/**.fsi`, the delta is `SkillMirror.fsi` **+42/−0 and nothing else** — insertion-only, zero deletions, so no signature was removed or retyped, no existing public DU gained a case, and no public record gained a field; neither major-forcing row of SDD's change-class table fires. That entire +42 belongs to 7.3.0; **7.3.0 → 7.4.0 changes no `.fsi` at all**, so 7.4.0 is a minor because `api-surface/` is a consumer-observable capability [FS.GG.Rendering#1101](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/1101) pins as a floor, not because the .NET public API moved. **Feed state re-confirmed at flip time on both feeds and not from a cached read**: the org feed's live read reports newest 7.4.0; nuget.org's flat container ends 7.4.0 re-read with `Cache-Control: no-cache` and a cache-busting query, and its registration hive independently reports `upper: 7.4.0`, `listed: true`, published 2026-07-27T19:40:57Z. **The published artifact was opened rather than inferred from a green run** — the 7.4.0 `.nupkg` (nuget.org archive SHA-256 `6016fe371d638735036b13277df7bc91fb02b6f2b5f2232bc32f059dca2500e7`; the org-feed archive differs only by nuget.org's appended `.signature.p7s`) carries the six `api-surface/` entries beside `lib/net10.0/`, and its nuspec records `<repository … commit="4383b820e8bf8046e24780f3323edd0659200db4">`, binding the served package to the merge `version` names. `package-version` leads the feed nowhere, so FR-007 holds. Both `registry-feed-coherence` and `source-coherence` were RED on `main` before this flip and are greened by it, landing on this repo only ([#741](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/741)). Generated compatibility, architecture, profile, and consumer projections were regenerated; architecture shape is unaffected, and `registry/repos.lock` is byte-unchanged — `dependencies.yml` is not one of the hashed `kit:` sources. *Protocol note: the preceding registry transaction (PR [#1603](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/1603), which flipped `fsgg-contracts` 7.0.0 → 7.2.0, `fs-gg-ui-template` 0.23.0 → 0.24.0 and `coord-engine` 0.11.0 → 0.12.0) prepended no entry here, so this log skips from 2026-07-26 to this entry; that gap is a real one and is not backfilled by this item.*

- **2026-07-26** — **publish-before-flip: `fs-gg-ui-template` package 0.22.0 → 0.23.0** (owner `rendering`; refs [Rendering#1067](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/1067)/[PR#1079](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/pull/1079), [.github#1501](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1501), FR-007). This template-only MINOR makes an intentionally event-free feedback cycle machine-verifiable without fabricating a defect or positive pattern: the create-new schema-v1 activation receipt binds the cycle, exercised phases, evidence, UTC timestamp, and reason no event qualified, while event JSONL and a zero-event receipt are mutually exclusive. Complete-state validation distinguishes valid eventful and verified-zero-event states from missing, empty, malformed, unreadable, or dual state. It binds every event record to the requested cycle and filename; restricts receipts to the exact seven-field allowlist; rejects duplicate, event-only, and arbitrary properties; rejects private absolute-path or secret-bearing evidence; and canonicalizes both receipt and event paths so symlinks cannot escape the workspace. Merge `ee5e6c376b9fd13a1ea5815f9aa7fc6b6218e331` and green release-tags run [30219888922](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/actions/runs/30219888922) preceded this flip. The ordered `fs-gg-ui-template/v0.23.0` → `v0.23.0` tags both resolve exactly to that merge; the framework snapshot stays `fs-gg-ui/v0.21.1` at `9b1824e12f92fe53bb4fecd9c556a8f0d49a05f1`, with no 0.23.0 framework tag or package. GitHub Packages record `1068615496` and nuget.org independently serve Template 0.23.0; excluding only nuget.org's `.signature.p7s`, all 1,870 payload paths and bytes match (served archive SHA-256 org `299d5df19ae3c91f6731194d6f4fe12969c354ce61873ef1618630baff78d5fb`, public `3d7af69b47c780f8ce52a00be27c58dc4d800031a9cd4b87958bed9b2404c72e`). The nuspec records the exact merge, the materialized feedback skill matches its manifest digest, and the payload's canonical `FsGgUiVersion` plus shipped symbology recipe references remain 0.21.1. Generated compatibility, architecture, profile, and consumer projections remain current; architecture shape is unaffected. FS.GG.Templates is deliberately untouched in this registry transaction.

- **2026-07-26** — **atomic publish-before-flip: Game receipt 0.12.0 + Rendering framework 0.21.0** (owners `game`, `rendering`; refs [Game#509](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Game/issues/509)/[PR#511](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Game/pull/511), [.github#1487](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1487), FR-007). Game now exports `journeyReceipt.schemaVersion: 1` only from an opaque, runner-issued production-journey proof and binds it to a same-execution generated JUnit report. The receipt carries producer composition authority; non-empty route, scenario, test, input, and terminal-predicate identities; execution id and receipt binding; lifecycle digests; bounded non-zero execution; and the passing terminal outcome. Authority-less loaders, caller-authored provenance, caller-supplied journey reports, mid-game external composition, terminal-at-boot proofs, mismatches, and aliased output paths fail closed. Rendering's published template consumer remains truthfully pinned to Game 0.10.1. Its full 18-package set moved under the ordered tag triple `fs-gg-ui/v0.21.0` → `fs-gg-ui-template/v0.21.0` → `v0.21.0`, all resolving to merge `0eae90aaf4d061eb89438dc35ca74623dc313ea7`; canonical run 30211542144 and escape-hatch run 30211554510 are green, and both feeds serve payload-identical packages excluding signing. This Rendering flip is atomic with Game because either proper subset leaves the aggregate live-feed gate red; it supersedes bot PR #1488. Generated compatibility, architecture, profile, and consumer projections were refreshed; architecture shape is unaffected.

- **2026-07-26** — **publish-before-flip: Game contracts 0.10.1 → 0.11.0 and production-journey adoption sequence** (owner `game`; refs [Game#507](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Game/issues/507)/[PR#508](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Game/pull/508), [.github#1484](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/1484), FR-007). - **2026-07-14** — **`shared-build-config` 1.0.0 → 1.1.0 — the drift check compares against a PIN, not against `main`, so an edit here stops red-lighting every open PR in every adopting repo**

  (owner `github`; [#592](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/592), [ADR-0036](../docs/adr/0036-the-build-config-drift-check-pins-its-source.md); refs [#499](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/499), [#536](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/536), [#626](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/626), FS.GG.SDD#379/#380)

  **The defect was a RACE, not a drift check.** Every `receives: build-config` repo runs a `Shared-build-config drift check` in its own `gate.yml` that checks `FS-GG/.github` out at **`ref: main`** and runs `sync-build-config.sh --check`, requiring byte-identity with `dist/dotnet/` **as of that checkout**. In FS.GG.SDD that check is **required**. So the verdict of a merge-blocking check was **a function of another repo's moving branch, evaluated whenever CI happened to run**: a receiver could not make it green **from its own PR**, and the instant anything landed in `dist/dotnet/` here, **every open PR in every adopting repo went red** — through no fault of its own, with no change to its branch.

  **It fired twice.** [#499](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/499) moved `Directory.Build.props` (ADR-0032) and FS.GG.SDD could merge **nothing** for hours — a finished, green PR sat blocked (SDD#379). [#536](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/536) then edited **the same file's XML comment** — correcting prose, no MSBuild element changed — and turned SDD#380 **red mid-flight**, between two pushes, because the target moved underneath it. **A comment was enough.** `gate.yml` had named its own escape condition (*"Revisit only if upstream churn causes flakiness"*); churn had.

  **The push arm was necessary and NOT SUFFICIENT.** [#626](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/626)'s `build-config-propagate.yml` (rolling, auto-merging sync PR per receiver) ended a *different* defect — the fabric was a ratchet, enforcing an update nothing ever pushed — and it shortened the freeze. It could not remove it: the window remained, and the freeze stayed **indefinitely reachable**, because a sync PR whose own `gate` job goes red (a config change that moves the restore graph needs a lockfile regenerated in the same PR) never auto-merges. That workflow's own fail-closed classifier exists to shout about exactly that state.

  **THE CHANGE.** The receiver now commits **`.config/fsgg-build-config.sha`** — the `.github` commit its managed files were synced from — and `--check` diffs them against **that commit's** `dist/dotnet/`. The verdict becomes a **pure function of the receiver's own tree** (its files + its pin, which arrive together on the PR's merge ref); nothing `.github` does to `main` can move it. **Behind → GREEN** (a loud `NOTICE`; the bot's rolling PR closes the gap and bumps files+pin in one diff). **Hand-edited → RED** (a defect the author can actually fix from the branch they are on — the only thing a required check can honestly demand). A deliberate inversion: for a *required* check, **stale-until-someone-merges beats merge-freeze-by-default**.

  **ADDITIVE / backward-compatible, hence MINOR.** An **unpinned** receiver is bit-for-bit unchanged — an absent pin means *legacy mode*, the old compare-against-`main` — so no receiver `gate.yml` changes, no flag day, no per-repo adoption item, and **the rollout cannot merge-freeze anyone**. That mattered more than usual here: a migration that froze the org would be this very defect, committed by its own fix. The pin is deliberately **not** in the syncer's `FILES` list (a *missing* member of `FILES` is DRIFT, so listing it would red-light every unpinned receiver on day one), and an **unresolvable** pin **fails open, loudly**. Additive growth is still growth ([ADR-0015](../docs/adr/0015-register-the-registry-schema-as-a-governed-contract.md)), so the minor bumps rather than riding along silently.

  **No receiver change was needed** because the receivers pull **both** the source files **and the checking script** from `.github@main` — so `--check`'s semantics are `.github`'s to define unilaterally. Consequence accepted with eyes open: **staleness is now possible and green.** Currency is enforced by `build-config-propagate.yml`, whose failure now means *"a repo is drifting out of date"* rather than *"a repo cannot merge"*. No staleness bound (it would reintroduce a failure depending on time and on `.github`'s commit rate rather than on the receiver's tree — the exact class being removed). Guarded by `tests/sync-build-config/run.sh`, whose #592 legs build a throwaway `.github` with real history and reproduce #536's comment-only edit: they go **red** against the pre-fix script.

- **2026-07-14** — **`fs-gg-ui-template` 0.9.0 → 0.9.2 — flipping to the release that actually shipped, and recording `0.9.1` as a PHANTOM** (owner rendering; refs [FS.GG.Rendering#684](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/pull/684), [#679](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/679), [#672](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/pull/672), [#587](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/587), [#642](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/642), [FS.GG.Game#219](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Game/issues/219), [.github#267](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/267), [#250](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/250), FR-007). Flipped `fs-gg-ui-template` `version` **and** `package-version` **0.9.0 → 0.9.2** in lockstep (FRAMEWORK release, pin == package == 0.9.2) and `package-tag` → `fs-gg-ui-template/v0.9.2`.

  **This entry is a late flip, not a release.** `0.9.2` went live on the org feed + nuget.org on **2026-07-13**; this registry was never flipped, so **publish-before-flip (FR-007) step 2 was missed** — the [#250](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/250) class, again. `feed-coherence` ([#267](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/267)) had been **RED on `main` ever since**, correctly reporting `package-version` BEHIND the feed. The gate did its job; nobody flipped the row. Its **daily schedule** is the only reason this was visible at all — a release that publishes without touching a file here changes nothing in this repo, so no PR-triggered check would ever have fired.

  **`0.9.2` is a FRAMEWORK release, not a template-only cut.** Verified package-by-package against the org feed rather than inferred from the template: all 18 `FS.GG.UI.*` members carry `0.9.2` (`FS.GG.UI.Canvas 0.9.2` present on **both** feeds), and the sibling library snapshot tag `fs-gg-ui/v0.9.2` exists. The **libraries moved** ⇒ framework. It carries the additive surface [#587](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/587) — `Persistence.interpretRecordOnly` alongside the still-present `[<Obsolete>] interpret`, `Viewer.runAppWithPersistence`, `AdapterCmd.diagnostics`, `ControlsElmish.audioRequests`, `SkiaViewer ViewerEffect.Persist` — which breaks the #642/Game#219 deadlock. ApiCompat vs published `0.9.0`: `OK=17 BREAK=0`, no suppressions. Bump `88f8ae2c`; release-tags run [29242305613](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/actions/runs/29242305613), **all six jobs green**, org-feed push and nuget.org OIDC dual-publish both succeeded.

  **⚠️ `0.9.1` IS A PHANTOM — it must never be pinned, and this is the entry that says so.** Its **full tag triple was cut** (`fs-gg-ui/v0.9.1` → `fs-gg-ui-template/v0.9.1` → `v0.9.1`, all at `1fddbd0b`) and **all three are still live on the remote** — yet **no `0.9.1` package exists on any feed**: absent from the org feed, and nuget.org's flatcontainer returns **404**. Not published-then-unlisted (an unlisted version still appears in the registration index; there is no `0.9.1` entry to unlist) — **never published at all**. The mechanism: release-tags run [29237248552](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/actions/runs/29237248552) pushed the tags from a `cut` job that **succeeded**, then **both** release-only gate jobs failed — the [#514](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/514) `Package.Tests` fixture leaked `FS_GG_VERSION_COHERENCE_RELEASE_LANE=1` into its `dotnet fsi` subprocess (disabling the RELEASE-PENDING waivers, so it demanded tags for its own synthetic `999.0.0`), and the generated-product gate compared `ViewerEffect list` values though `ViewerEffect` has no structural equality (`ViewerDiagnosticsOptions.Sink` is a function reached via `StartBoundedRun`) ⇒ FS0001 in the template payload. `publish-packages` was then **correctly skipped** by its `needs:`. **The tags outlived the publish.** Rendering's own pin named `0.9.1` while no feed served it, so every subsequent PR there went red with `NU1102`, and reverting was unavailable (`fs-gg-ui/v0.9.1` was already pushed and `pin-lags-tag` is asserted with no waiver) — `0.9.2` (PR #684, `fix(#679)`, "unwedge main") is the escape, delivering 0.9.1's payload **and** fixing the two bugs that aborted it.

  **The rule this establishes:** **a tag is not a publish.** `package-tag` names the tag of the coherent set a row's `package-version` was **published from** — a tag alone never earns it, and any tooling that infers "published" from "a tag exists" is *wrong about 0.9.1* today. Both fields now say so at the scalar.

- **2026-07-14** — **`skill-registry` — an OPTIONAL ADDITIVE field IS schema growth, and ADR-0015's bump procedure was unrunnable** (owner github; refs [.github#686](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/686), [#658](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/658)/[PR #687](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/pull/687), [#689](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/689), [FS.GG.SDD#420](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.SDD/issues/420), ADR-0015, ADR-0017, ADR-0022 §6). No version scalar moves in this entry — it records a **decision** and a **debt**. (1) **DECIDED:** an optional, additive, back-compatible field **is** schema growth. The tempting "it's optional, no reader breaks, so it doesn't bump" is exactly the reasoning ADR-0015 was written to refuse — its motivating defect (M5) *was* additive growth: the registry "grew one-off fields" (`minimum-fsgg-sdd`, `package-tag`, `profiles`) and "because the typed validator treats most fields as optional (**additive tolerance**), a stale validator silently degrades toward a YAML-parses check." Back-compat is why the drift is **silent**, hence why it must be **tracked**; ADR-0015's Consequences make it the ambient condition, not the test ("unknown fields are still tolerated"). The test is: *does the on-disk schema now carry a field or rule the pinned validator does not know?* (2) **DEBT RECORDED:** `mirrored:` grew `skills.yml` in #658 and landed unbumped, so `schemaVersion` 1 → 2 + `skill-registry.version` 1 → 2 is **OWED**. It is deliberately **unpaid**: `Fsgg.Registry` does not yet assert over `skills.yml` **at all**, so bumping now would version a schema no validator reads and pin a CLI carrying nothing — the [#416](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/416) ornament, inside the contract registry. (3) **PROCEDURE CORRECTED:** ADR-0015 §3's "bump in the **SAME PR** that teaches the validator" is **unsatisfiable for both governed schema contracts** — the schema *document* lives in `.github` (`skills.yml` / this file) and the *validator* (`Fsgg.Registry`) lives in `FS.GG.SDD`; no PR spans two repos. It never bit because no `schemaVersion` had ever been bumped — `mirrored` is the first real schema growth since ADR-0015 and it exposed the procedure cannot be run. Replaced, in the `skill-registry` contract row + this projection, by the org's own **publish-before-flip** (FR-007): FS.GG.SDD#420 teaches `Fsgg.Registry` (`mirrored` is an OPTIONAL boolean whose **absent must never coerce to `false`** — that coercion is the fail-open #658 exists to close) and publishes a CLI; `.github` **then** bumps both scalars and advances the pin. Amending ADR-0015 itself is #689 (this repo amends an Accepted ADR with a new one, cf. ADR-0027/ADR-0021 — never in place). Also names `mirrored` in the contract's `surface:`, which under-described the document it governs.
- **2026-07-11** — **`fs-gg-ui-template` 0.8.0 → 0.9.0 — the FS.GG.UI `0.9.0` framework MINOR that finally publishes the interactive audio seam** (owner rendering; refs [FS.GG.Rendering#492](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/492), [PR #498](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/pull/498), [#429](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/429)/[PR #433](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/pull/433), [#438](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/438)/[PR #486](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/pull/486), [#436](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/436)). Flipped `fs-gg-ui-template` `version` **and** `package-version` **0.8.0 → 0.9.0** in lockstep (FRAMEWORK release, pin == package == 0.9.0) and `package-tag` → `fs-gg-ui-template/v0.9.0`.

  **Why a release was owed at all.** `ControlsElmish.runInteractiveAppWithAudio` / `runInteractiveAppWithWindowBehaviorAndAudio` (#429) and the scripted interactive runners' audio sink (#438) both merged **after** `fs-gg-ui/v0.8.0`. The seam therefore existed in `src/`, shipped in **no published package**, and was unreachable from every scaffolded product: the template pinned 0.8.0, which has no audio-capable launcher, so a pointer-aware product still got **silence** — the exact complaint #429 was filed about. #436's template-side wiring could not compile against it. A seam nobody can call is not a seam yet.

  **This is a sequencing gap CI structurally cannot catch:** `src/` compiles against itself, while the gates that build the *scaffolded product* build it against the *published pin*. So the framework can grow public API its own template provably cannot consume, and nothing goes red. Filed as [FS.GG.Rendering#504](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/504) (no gate asserts the template can consume the framework API it calls *at the pinned version* — the mirror tracks `src/`, the template compiles against the pin), and the release window's always-red pin gate as [#506](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/506).

  Additive only ⇒ MINOR on the 0.x line; the required ApiCompat gate passed against the published 0.8.0 baseline with **no suppression** (contrast 0.6.0/0.7.0, which needed transient `IsBaselineSuppression`). Cut by the automated `release-tags.yml` on bump commit `893757b5` (run [29157844670](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/actions/runs/29157844670)): tags `fs-gg-ui/v0.9.0` → `fs-gg-ui-template/v0.9.0` → `v0.9.0`, both release-only gates (package-consumption, template-instantiation) green, then publish. **publish-before-flip (FR-007) satisfied** — `FS.GG.UI.Controls.Elmish 0.9.0` verified newest on the **org feed** before this row flipped; the nuget.org OIDC dual-publish returned `Created` for every member ("Published coherent set 0.9.0 to nuget.org"), and its flatcontainer index lagged the push by minutes — indexing latency, not a failed publish. - **2026-07-10** — **`skill-registry-published`: producer condition (2) OPEN → CLEARED; the row stays `coherent: false` on a `.github`-side step alone** (owner github; refs [.github#290](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/290), epic [.github#266](https://github.com/FS-GG/.github/issues/266), [FS.GG.Rendering#77](https://github.com/FS-GG/FS.GG.Rendering/issues/77), ADR-0017). A **note correction, not a state change**: `coherent` remains `false`.

Rendering#77 **closed 2026-07-04** (`cf93eabd`). The note, its `resolved_by`/`impact`/`tracking` fields, the `skill-registry` contract's `validator` prose, the `github → rendering` dependency edge, `registry/skills.yml`'s header, [`docs/architecture.md`](../docs/architecture.md) and the [compatibility projection](../docs/registry/compatibility.md) **all** repeated the "blocked ONLY on Rendering#77" claim. The row was being held open by a note describing a defect fixed six days earlier.

So `registry = manifest = evaluable` — the property condition (2) demanded — already holds.
